## Current KV client fix (2026-10-18)

Unused denial streams are cancelled without awaiting cancellation; private-state withdrawal and the typed denial do not wait for a hostile or stalled body.

Workbench and administration detect 401/403 from the response status before consuming an error body, preserving their existing private-state clearing when an intermediary returns invalid or unreadable JSON. Non-authentication failures still retain bounded recovery metadata where available; a `null` workbench error body now produces its proper sanitized status instead of a TypeError. This is a new client-source change relative to the retained 9cb/d901 qualifications; those historical full-build, CI, image and browser results are not relabelled as qualification of this fix. The two existing client suites pass all 21 tests on Node 22, including malformed-denial and no-retry regressions. Server contracts, native limits and the original acceptance records are unchanged.

Twelve warm samples p50/p95 14.45/16.37 ms made zero CLI calls; twenty concurrent cold requests shared one call. Initial-outage repeated requests returned 503 with no additional calls during backoff. After each behavior change, every docs domain is checked and affected contracts updated. Focused regressions and relevant unit/type/build/HTTP/browser checks accompany each stage. Tests use disposable SQLite, fake credentials/CLI and loopback mock/unavailable native APIs. No operator database, live kubeconfig, production value or production mutation is used. Broad checks are not repeated on an unchanged stage.

A command combining shutdown and recursive cleanup of an owned UI fixture was rejected by automatic approval review. Narrow shutdown of the two verified disposable processes succeeded; its temporary files were retained. Stdin-controlled fixtures normally stop and clean up. The KV browser fixture's non-PTY stdin had closed before cleanup; its three verified child processes were stopped separately and its synthetic temporary files retained. The earlier blocked historical prototype is never retried or bypassed.

## Native Secrets engine scope

Parallel UI integration contract: the root owns `/secrets/kv` entrypoint, metadata browser/forms/shared-query whitelist and the unified ledger. The workbench component is named/default `KvWorkbench` in `src/components/secrets/kv-workbench.tsx`, with required `path={id,mountId,path,namespace,name,revision,fields:[{field,key}]}`, `capabilities` (fresh capability result or null), `onClose()` and `onMetadataRefresh()`; `returnFocus()` is optional. Mount the accessible dialog only for the selected path. It subscribes to fresh session/capabilities, owns private ephemeral values, and sends captured pathRevision/head/resourceVersion on native mutations. `KvAdministration` accepts optional `{selectedPathId, refreshKey, active, initialOpen, onClose, onMetadataChange(scope)}` as defined in its integration guide; the parent callback refreshes metadata without resetting the just-issued local token. Callbacks invalidate metadata only; never pass values, token credentials or client-role assertions between components. All server permissions remain authoritative. The engine authorizes document replacement under update policy independently of read policy: a separate explicit replacement mode works without prefetched values and explicitly acknowledges omitted mapped fields. Preserve-existing-values editing requires read; no-read replacement must not grant it. Broader final interaction qualification remains open. Package/lock/Dockerfile maintenance is a separate executor scope. Qualified source commits must be integrated and tested together before final image/recovery/live handoff.

Fixture isolation: the UI and all three HTTP fixtures use loopback listen(0); the Next child reserves an ephemeral port rather than a shared fixed range. Every run uses a unique mkdtemp SQLite/native-fixture directory and owned child processes. The final UI gateways were 62797/62798/62799; all owned tabs/processes were stopped and their temporary directory removed. Parallel worktrees must keep separate build outputs and stop only verified owned resources; there is no global Node/container cleanup or reduced HTTP assertion set.

Source tracking: the broad k8s-rewrite secrets-directory ignore keeps real Secret/runtime directories ignored. Only four intended component/model source paths under front-end/src/components/secrets are excepted: kv-workbench.tsx, kv-workbench-model.ts, kv-administration.tsx and kv-administration-model.ts. Other files in that namespace remain ignored, including credential JSON/environment/key files and arbitrary source names. This tracking correction has no application behavior or credential migration.

Application-wide confidentiality follow-up is active: the existing native Flux credential writer and retained-cascade behavior were source-verified, but repository URL userinfo could enter metadata SQLite/manifests. URL validation now rejects credential-bearing URL containers before side effects; public projections withhold unsafe existing URLs and raw controller output. All 89 unit tests, lint, fresh build/typecheck and both HTTP fixtures passed; see [Flux confidentiality evidence](implementation-evidence/flux-confidentiality.json). The optional local runner now configures a fixed producer callback and discards arbitrary stdout/stderr before job storage; local producer/lifecycle/persistence/stream qualification passed with 94 unit tests, zero-warning lint, fresh build/typecheck and three production HTTP fixtures. See [provisioning evidence](implementation-evidence/provisioning-confidentiality.json); deployed Linux runner/image and historical-output review remain separate. Container provisioning stays disabled and external no_log requirements remain. No application-wide confidentiality completion is claimed.

The qualified flat editor stage addresses A17: selection/reveal/clear/save leave no replacement intent; intentional empty values use an explicit checkbox; key and alias deletions require exact metadata confirmation. Direct requests use the bounded shared lifecycle and fence late values after clearing, permission loss or navigation. All 14 focused regressions, zero-warning lint, a fresh production build/typecheck and browser interactions passed using the owned UI fixture's opt-in `BORTUS_UI_NATIVE_FIXTURE=true` CA-verified mock API. Its controls and assertions remain synthetic and never mount production credentials or expose values in fixture status.

The concrete [architecture/API implementation contract](BORTUS-KV-DESIGN.md) specifies scalar/group mapping, native current/history ownership, metadata whitelists, candidate/commit CAS, ambiguous recovery, drift, retention/destruction/GC, policies and token audiences, migrations/env/RBAC, 603 legacy aliases, binary-safe import/export and consumer rotation intent. The different-key last-applied annotation regression must extend into the UI as it is added.

Values/history stay native; SQLite holds metadata and authentication hashes, never secret values or value digests. Readonly stays metadata-only. Current objects preserve unrelated keys/type/metadata; owned history/auth metadata is minimal. TLS, resourceVersion and fresh account/policy gates remain mandatory. Bootstrap runs externally and cannot depend on Secrets in the cluster being created.

### Terminal dock contract and qualification history
The terminal is a named nonmodal region anchored to the viewport's current content width, including sidebar resize. Restore and switch use buttons; height is adjustable with ArrowUp/ArrowDown and Home/End on its named separator. The active command input receives focus and uses xterm5.3 screenReaderMode supporting DOM. Closing the last user-opened pod terminal returns focus to its originating Actions button when still connected. Minimize disposes the private terminal and input/history; its bar exposes Restore/Maximize/Close and no misleading Clear. Active Clear resets buffer and input/history, rewrites the public welcome/prompt and focuses input. Hidden/pagehide/navigation and fresh permission/runtime withdrawal close terminals; command ownership aborts and late replies remain fenced by unmount. Initialization owns its animation frame and terminal disposer independently of a detached DOM ref. A loading status covers the deferred chunk. At that draft point, these changes had not yet passed fresh build/browser qualification and do not establish real AT or image acceptance. See https://xtermjs.org/docs/api/terminal/interfaces/iterminaloptions/ and https://xtermjs.org/docs/api/terminal/classes/terminal/.

These local contribution observations will not imply source speed improvement, full AT or image acceptance.

### Longhorn overview health classification

The overview Longhorn healthy-volume count requires explicit `status.robustness === "healthy"`. Attachment (`status.state`) is a separate display attribute: attached degraded, faulted, unknown or missing robustness never counts as healthy; detached or creating records with explicit healthy robustness do. Total inventory and attachment display are unchanged. Labelled retained snapshots keep their original counts on failure; once unavailable, no healthy inventory is invented. This follows the distinct [Longhorn state/robustness definitions](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/longhorn/longhorn-manager/master/k8s/pkg/apis/longhorn/v1beta2/volume.go); upstream source is semantic context, not live cluster evidence.
